```
3	import { useEffect, useState } from "react";
4	import { authClient, currentUser, ensureSignedIn, markOnlineUsed, onlineUsed, type OnlineUser } from "@/lib/auth/client";
5	
6	/**
7	 * The account corner of the menu: shows who you are online, offers instant
8	 * guest play, and upgrades a guest to a real account without losing worlds
9	 * (the server re-parents them — see lib/auth/server.ts). Purely additive to
10	 * the offline game: with no online features touched, no account ever exists.
11	 */
12	export default function AccountPanel() {
13	  const [user, setUser] = useState<OnlineUser | null>(null);
14	  const [mode, setMode] = useState<"closed" | "signin" | "signup">("closed");
15	  const [email, setEmail] = useState("");
16	  const [password, setPassword] = useState("");
17	  const [name, setName] = useState("");
18	  const [busy, setBusy] = useState(false);
19	  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
20	
21	  useEffect(() => {
22	    // Offline-first: never even ask the server about a session until this
23	    // browser has used online features once (see lib/auth/client.ts).
24	    if (onlineUsed()) void currentUser().then(setUser);
25	  }, []);
26	
27	  const refresh = async () => setUser(await currentUser());
28	
29	  const submit = async (event: React.FormEvent) => {
30	    event.preventDefault();
31	    setBusy(true);
32	    setError(null);
33	    markOnlineUsed();
34	    // Sign-up (and sign-in) while holding a guest session links the account:
35	    // the guest's worlds move over server-side before the guest is deleted.
36	    try {
37	      const result =
38	        mode === "signup"
39	          ? await authClient().signUp.email({ email, password, name: name.trim() || email.split("@")[0] })
40	          : await authClient().signIn.email({ email, password });
41	      if (result.error) {
42	        setError(result.error.message ?? "That didn't work — check the details and try again.");
43	        return;
44	      }
45	      setMode("closed");
46	      setPassword("");
47	      await refresh();
48	    } catch {
49	      // A thrown request (network failure) instead of an { error } result.
50	      setError("Couldn't reach the server — check your connection and try again.");
51	    } finally {
52	      setBusy(false);
53	    }
54	  };
55	
56	  if (mode !== "closed") {
57	    return (
58	      <form className="menu-form account-panel" onSubmit={submit}>
59	        <label className="menu-field">
60	          Email
61	          <input className="menu-input" type="email" required value={email} onChange={(e) => setEmail(e.target.value)} />
62	        </label>
63	        {mode === "signup" && (
64	          <label className="menu-field">
65	            Display name
66	            <input className="menu-input" value={name} maxLength={24} onChange={(e) => setName(e.target.value)} />
67	          </label>
68	        )}
69	        <label className="menu-field">
70	          Password
71	          <input className="menu-input" type="password" required minLength={8} value={password} onChange={(e) => setPassword(e.target.value)} />
72	        </label>
73	        {error && <div className="account-error">{error}</div>}
74	        <div className="menu-form-actions">
75	          <button type="submit" className="mc-button" disabled={busy}>
76	            {mode === "signup" ? (user?.isAnonymous ? "Create account (keeps your worlds)" : "Create account") : "Sign in"}
77	          </button>
78	          <button type="button" className="mc-button" onClick={() => setMode(mode === "signup" ? "signin" : "signup")}>
79	            {mode === "signup" ? "I have an account" : "I need an account"}
80	          </button>
81	          <button type="button" className="mc-button" onClick={() => setMode("closed")}>
82	            Cancel
83	          </button>
84	        </div>
85	      </form>
86	    );
87	  }
88	
89	  return (
90	    <div className="account-panel">
91	      {user ? (
92	        <>
93	          <span className="account-status">{user.isAnonymous ? "Playing as guest" : `Signed in as ${user.name}`}</span>
94	          {user.isAnonymous ? (
95	            <button type="button" className="mc-button" onClick={() => setMode("signup")}>
96	              Keep my worlds — create account
97	            </button>
98	          ) : (
99	            <button
100	              type="button"
101	              className="mc-button"
102	              onClick={async () => {
103	                await authClient().signOut();
104	                await refresh();
105	              }}
106	            >
107	              Sign out
108	            </button>
109	          )}
110	        </>
111	      ) : (
112	        <>
113	          <span className="account-status">Offline</span>
114	          <button
115	            type="button"
116	            className="mc-button"
117	            onClick={async () => {
118	              setBusy(true);
119	              setError(null);
120	              try {
121	                await ensureSignedIn();
122	                await refresh();
123	              } catch {
124	                setError("Couldn't reach the server — check your connection and try again.");
125	              } finally {
126	                setBusy(false);
127	              }
128	            }}
129	            disabled={busy}
130	          >
131	            Play online as guest
132	          </button>
133	          <button type="button" className="mc-button" onClick={() => setMode("signin")}>
134	            Sign in
135	          </button>
136	          {error && <div className="account-error">{error}</div>}
137	        </>
138	      )}
139	    </div>
140	  );
141	}
```

```
1	"use client";
2	
3	import { createAuthClient } from "better-auth/react";
4	import { anonymousClient } from "better-auth/client/plugins";
5	
6	/**
7	 * Browser-side auth. Guests are created LAZILY — only when the player takes
8	 * an online action (sign-in panel, cloud sync, joining a world). Plain
9	 * offline single-player never calls any of this, so it stays account-free.
10	 *
11	 * The client itself constructs lazily too: better-auth validates the base
12	 * URL at construction, which must not run at import time (component tests
13	 * import menu modules under happy-dom, where no real origin exists).
14	 */
15	type Client = ReturnType<typeof createAuthClient<{ plugins: [ReturnType<typeof anonymousClient>] }>>;
16	
17	let instance: Client | null = null;
18	
19	export function authClient(): Client {
20	  instance ??= createAuthClient({
21	    baseURL: window.location.origin,
22	    plugins: [anonymousClient()]
23	  });
24	  return instance;
25	}
26	
27	export type OnlineUser = {
28	  id: string;
29	  name: string;
30	  email: string;
31	  isAnonymous: boolean;
32	};
33	
34	/**
35	 * "Has this browser ever used online features?" Offline-first hinges on it:
36	 * until the player takes an online action, the menu makes NO auth requests —
37	 * so a dev/e2e server without a database never even sees a session fetch.
38	 */
39	const ONLINE_USED_KEY = "minecraft_online_v1";
40	
41	export function onlineUsed(storage: Storage = localStorage): boolean {
42	  return storage.getItem(ONLINE_USED_KEY) === "1";
43	}
44	
45	export function markOnlineUsed(storage: Storage = localStorage): void {
46	  storage.setItem(ONLINE_USED_KEY, "1");
47	}
48	
49	/** The signed-in user (guest or full), or null. */
50	export async function currentUser(): Promise<OnlineUser | null> {
51	  const { data } = await authClient().getSession();
52	  if (!data?.user) return null;
53	  const user = data.user as { id: string; name: string; email: string; isAnonymous?: boolean | null };
54	  return { id: user.id, name: user.name, email: user.email, isAnonymous: user.isAnonymous === true };
55	}
56	
57	/** Ensures some identity exists — signs in anonymously on first online action. */
58	export async function ensureSignedIn(): Promise<OnlineUser | null> {
59	  markOnlineUsed();
60	  const existing = await currentUser();
61	  if (existing) return existing;
62	  const { error } = await authClient().signIn.anonymous();
63	  if (error) return null;
64	  return currentUser();
65	}
66	
```

```
3	import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";
4	import MinecraftGame from "@/components/MinecraftGame";
5	import ProfileSelect from "@/components/menu/ProfileSelect";
6	import WorldSelect from "@/components/menu/WorldSelect";
7	import { ensureSignedIn } from "@/lib/auth/client";
8	import { migrateLegacySave } from "@/lib/game/legacyMigration";
9	import { getProfile, setActiveProfile } from "@/lib/game/profiles";
10	import { createWorld, deleteWorld, getWorld, touchWorld, worldSaveKey, type WorldMeta } from "@/lib/game/worlds";
11	import { writeSave } from "@/lib/game/save";
12	import { pullCloudSaveIfNewer } from "@/lib/game/cloudSaves";
13	import { deleteOnlineWorld, requestJoinTicket, type OnlineWorld } from "@/lib/online/onlineClient";
14	import { connectNetworkSession, type NetworkSession } from "@/lib/net/NetworkSession";
15	import { installUiTiles } from "@/lib/ui/chromeTiles";
16	
17	/**
18	 * Top-level menu shell. Owns the screen state machine (profile-select ->
19	 * world-select -> play) and boots the legacy migration once on mount. The play
20	 * screen mounts MinecraftGame keyed by world id + a reload nonce, so switching
21	 * worlds (or Load/Reset) remounts the subtree — the game effect's cleanup
22	 * disposes the old engine/renderer and a fresh mount boots the next world, with
23	 * no page reload.
24	 */
25	type Screen =
26	  | { name: "profile-select" }
27	  | { name: "world-select"; profileId: string }
28	  | { name: "play"; profileId: string; worldId: string }
29	  | { name: "play-online"; profileId: string; world: OnlineWorld; session: NetworkSession };
30	
31	/** Online worlds mount the same game subtree; the meta is a projection of the server row. */
32	function onlineWorldMeta(world: OnlineWorld, profileId: string): WorldMeta {
33	  return {
34	    id: `online:${world.id}`,
35	    profileId,
36	    name: world.name,
37	    seed: world.seed,
38	    worldType: world.worldType as WorldMeta["worldType"],
39	    gameMode: world.gameMode as WorldMeta["gameMode"],
40	    difficulty: world.difficulty as WorldMeta["difficulty"],
41	    hardcore: world.hardcore,
42	    worldgenVersion: world.worldgenVersion,
43	    createdAt: 0,
44	    lastPlayedAt: 0
45	  };
46	}
47	
48	/**
49	 * Remembers the world being played for this tab so a reload resumes it instead
50	 * of dropping back to the menu. sessionStorage (not localStorage) so a brand-new
51	 * tab still cold-starts at profile-select.
52	 */
53	const SESSION_KEY = "monecraft_active_session";
54	
55	function readSessionPointer(): { profileId: string; worldId: string } | null {
56	  try {
57	    const raw = sessionStorage.getItem(SESSION_KEY);
58	    if (!raw) return null;
59	    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as { profileId?: unknown; worldId?: unknown };
60	    if (typeof parsed?.profileId === "string" && typeof parsed?.worldId === "string") {
61	      return { profileId: parsed.profileId, worldId: parsed.worldId };
62	    }
63	    return null;
64	  } catch {
65	    return null;
66	  }
67	}
68	
69	function writeSessionPointer(pointer: { profileId: string; worldId: string } | null): void {
70	  try {
71	    if (pointer) sessionStorage.setItem(SESSION_KEY, JSON.stringify(pointer));
72	    else sessionStorage.removeItem(SESSION_KEY);
73	  } catch {
74	    // No resume across reload if sessionStorage is unavailable — never fatal.
75	  }
76	}
77	
78	export default function GameShell() {
79	  const [ready, setReady] = useState(false);
80	  const [screen, setScreen] = useState<Screen>({ name: "profile-select" });
81	  const [reloadNonce, setReloadNonce] = useState(0);
82	  const [connecting, setConnecting] = useState<string | null>(null);
83	  const [connectError, setConnectError] = useState<string | null>(null);
84	  // A join is in flight — guards against a double-click opening (and leaking) a
85	  // second socket. A ref, not `connecting`, so it's set synchronously.
86	  const joiningRef = useRef(false);
87	
88	  /**
89	   * Open a world for play. A cloud-linked one (WorldMeta.cloudId) reconciles
90	   * first: pull the remote save if it advanced past this device's cursor, write
91	   * it to disk, and boot from disk like any other world — reusing the "Opening…"
92	   * gate. The caller owns the `joiningRef` guard (this helper does not), so the
93	   * materialize-then-open path can hold it across `createWorld` too.
94	   */
95	  const openWorld = async (profileId: string, worldId: string) => {
96	    const world = getWorld(worldId);
97	    touchWorld(worldId);
98	    writeSessionPointer({ profileId, worldId });
99	    if (world?.cloudId) {
100	      setConnecting(world.name);
101	      try {
102	        const decision = await pullCloudSaveIfNewer(world.cloudId);
103	        if (decision.adopt) writeSave(worldSaveKey(worldId), decision.save);
104	      } catch {
105	        // Offline or a bad blob → fall through and play the local copy.
106	      } finally {
107	        setConnecting(null);
108	      }
109	    }
110	    setScreen({ name: "play", profileId, worldId });
111	  };
112	
113	  const playLocal = async (profileId: string, worldId: string) => {
114	    if (joiningRef.current) return;
115	    joiningRef.current = true;
116	    try {
117	      await openWorld(profileId, worldId);
118	    } finally {
119	      joiningRef.current = false;
120	    }
121	  };
122	
123	  /** Materialize a cloud save as a local world (linked by cloudId), then open it — the pull-on-open fills it in. */
124	  const downloadCloud = async (profileId: string, world: OnlineWorld) => {
125	    // Guard BEFORE createWorld so a fast double-click can't persist two local
126	    // worlds sharing the same cloudId (the second open would just be dropped).
127	    if (joiningRef.current) return;
128	    joiningRef.current = true;
129	    try {
130	      const local = createWorld(profileId, world.name, String(world.seed), {
131	        worldType: world.worldType as WorldMeta["worldType"],
132	        gameMode: world.gameMode as WorldMeta["gameMode"],
133	        difficulty: world.difficulty as WorldMeta["difficulty"],
134	        hardcore: world.hardcore,
135	        worldgenVersion: world.worldgenVersion,
136	        cloudId: world.id
137	      });
138	      await openWorld(profileId, local.id);
139	    } finally {
140	      joiningRef.current = false;
141	    }
142	  };
143	
144	  /** Guest-or-account → ticket → socket → replica sync → play. */
145	  const playOnline = async (profileId: string, world: OnlineWorld) => {
146	    if (joiningRef.current) return; // a join is already in flight
147	    joiningRef.current = true;
148	    setConnectError(null);
149	    setConnecting(world.name);
150	    try {
151	      if (!(await ensureSignedIn())) throw new Error("sign-in failed");
152	      const grant = await requestJoinTicket(world.id);
153	      if (!grant) throw new Error("could not get a join ticket (is the game server configured?)");
154	      // The reconnect ladder mints a fresh short-lived ticket each retry — the
155	      // 60 s TTL means a stale one is useless a minute after a drop.
156	      const session = await connectNetworkSession(grant.gameServerUrl, grant.ticket, undefined, {
157	        reconnect: async () => {
158	          const next = await requestJoinTicket(world.id);
159	          return next ? { url: next.gameServerUrl, ticket: next.ticket } : null;
160	        }
161	      });
162	      setScreen({ name: "play-online", profileId, world, session });
163	    } catch (error) {
164	      setConnectError(error instanceof Error ? error.message : "connection failed");
165	    } finally {
166	      setConnecting(null);
167	      joiningRef.current = false;
168	    }
169	  };
170	
171	  useEffect(() => {
172	    installUiTiles(); // the menu chrome shares the in-game noise tiles
173	    migrateLegacySave();
174	    // Resume the tab's world if one was being played and still exists.
175	    const pointer = readSessionPointer();
176	    const resume: Screen | null =
177	      pointer && getProfile(pointer.profileId) && getWorld(pointer.worldId) ? { name: "play", profileId: pointer.profileId, worldId: pointer.worldId } : null;
178	    // Microtask hop keeps this off the synchronous effect path (cascading-render lint).
179	    queueMicrotask(() => {
180	      if (resume) setScreen(resume);
181	      setReady(true);
182	    });
183	  }, []);
184	
185	  // localStorage is read below; hold the neutral frame until we're client-side.
186	  if (!ready) return <div className="menu-screen" />;
187	
188	  if (screen.name === "play") {
189	    const profile = getProfile(screen.profileId);
190	    const world = getWorld(screen.worldId);
191	    // Both exist in normal flow; a cross-tab delete drops us back to a menu.
192	    if (profile && world) {
193	      return (
194	        <MinecraftGame
195	          key={`${world.id}:${reloadNonce}`}
196	          world={world}
197	          profile={profile}
198	          onQuitToWorlds={() => {
199	            writeSessionPointer(null);
200	            setScreen({ name: "world-select", profileId: profile.id });
201	          }}
202	          onDeleteWorld={() => {
203	            deleteWorld(world.id); // hardcore Game Over: erase the dead world and leave
204	            writeSessionPointer(null);
205	            setScreen({ name: "world-select", profileId: profile.id });
206	          }}
207	          onReloadWorld={() => setReloadNonce((nonce) => nonce + 1)}
208	        />
209	      );
210	    }
211	  }
212	
213	  if (screen.name === "play-online") {
214	    const profile = getProfile(screen.profileId);
215	    if (profile) {
216	      return (
217	        <MinecraftGame
218	          key={`online:${screen.world.id}`}
219	          world={onlineWorldMeta(screen.world, profile.id)}
220	          profile={profile}
221	          online={screen.session}
222	          onQuitToWorlds={() => setScreen({ name: "world-select", profileId: profile.id })}
223	          onDeleteWorld={() => {
224	            // Hardcore game-over: actually delete the shared world on the server
225	            // (owner-gated; a member just leaves), then return to the list.
226	            screen.session.dispose();
227	            void deleteOnlineWorld(screen.world.id);
228	            setScreen({ name: "world-select", profileId: profile.id });
229	          }}
230	          onReloadWorld={() => setScreen({ name: "world-select", profileId: profile.id })}
231	        />
232	      );
233	    }
234	  }
235	
236	  if (screen.name === "world-select") {
237	    const profile = getProfile(screen.profileId);
238	    if (profile) {
239	      return (
240	        <>
241	          <WorldSelect
242	            profile={profile}
243	            onPlay={(worldId) => void playLocal(profile.id, worldId)}
244	            onPlayOnline={(world) => void playOnline(profile.id, world)}
245	            onDownloadCloud={(world) => void downloadCloud(profile.id, world)}
246	            onBack={() => setScreen({ name: "profile-select" })}
247	          />
248	          {connecting && (
249	            <div className="net-modal" role="status">
250	              <div className="net-modal-box">Opening “{connecting}”…</div>
251	            </div>
252	          )}
253	          {connectError && (
254	            <div className="net-modal" role="alertdialog" aria-label="Connection failed">
255	              <div className="net-modal-box">
256	                <p>Couldn&apos;t join: {connectError}</p>
257	                <button type="button" className="mc-button" onClick={() => setConnectError(null)}>
258	                  OK
259	                </button>
260	              </div>
261	            </div>
262	          )}
263	        </>
264	      );
265	    }
266	  }
267	
268	  return (
269	    <ProfileSelect
270	      onPlay={(profileId) => {
271	        setActiveProfile(profileId);
272	        setScreen({ name: "world-select", profileId });
273	      }}
274	    />
275	  );
276	}
```

> AGENT

I now have a complete, precise picture. Let me confirm the two root causes before finalizing an approach:

**Problem 1 — forced profile creation before login.** In `components/menu/ProfileSelect.tsx:27`, the `firstRun` branch (zero profiles) renders `CreateProfileForm` full-screen and the `<AccountPanel />` (which holds *Sign in / Register / Play online as guest*) is only rendered in the profile-list view below it (`ProfileSelect.tsx:44`). So a brand-new user literally cannot reach login/register until a profile exists.

**Problem 2 — no way back to login from guest mode.** In `components/menu/AccountPanel.tsx:94-97`, a guest (`user.isAnonymous`) is offered *only* "Keep my worlds — create account" (→ sign-up). The `signOut()` call exists solely in the non-anonymous branch (`:103`), so a guest has no sign-out and no route back to the sign-in form.

One important architectural fact that shapes the fix: a **local profile (name + skin, localStorage)** and an **account (server identity)** are orthogonal — even after logging in you still need a local profile to actually play. So "login first" can't *replace* profile creation; the real fix is making login *accessible* rather than blocked. Two genuine product decisions remain, so let me confirm those with you.

> AGENT

Sure — what would you like to clarify before we lock these in?

A couple of things I can expand on that might be behind your hesitation:

- **On the architecture:** In the current design, a *profile* (local name + skin in your browser) and an *account* (server login) are deliberately separate things. Logging in doesn't create a profile, and you still need a profile to actually load into a world. So neither option makes login "replace" profile creation — they just stop login from being *blocked* behind it. If your mental model is that login should be the single front gate and the profile should be derived from / merged into the account, that's a third, larger direction we could discuss.

- **On "the order seems backwards":** I want to make sure I'm reading your intent right. Do you mean (a) login/register should simply be *reachable* on the very first screen (not hidden), or (b) login/register should genuinely come *first*, as the primary entry point, with profile/offline play secondary?

- **On guest exit / "the login screen":** When you say "get back to the login screen," do you mean the Sign in form specifically, or the general Offline state that shows *Sign in / Register / Play as guest*? And when a guest signs out, they'd lose access to any online worlds that guest created (unless they'd upgraded to keep them) — is a plain sign-out fine, or should it warn first?

What's on your mind?

> DEVELOPER

are profiles belongs to account, if it is a guest, maybe we do not need to create account, the profile can be store in local browser cookies or session, what do you think?

> AGENT

That's a good instinct, and it actually lines up with how the code is already shaped — let me connect it to what exists so we scope this well.

**Where the current model already agrees with you:**
- A **guest already needs no account for offline play**. The local profile (`lib/game/profiles.ts`, stored in `localStorage`) is just `{ name, skinId }` and owns local worlds. The server-side "anonymous" better-auth user is created *lazily* — only when a guest takes a genuinely online action (`ensureSignedIn()` in `lib/auth/client.ts:58`, called only on cloud sync / joining a world). So "guest = local profile in the browser, no account" is essentially already true today.
- Why the server guest exists at all: to *join an online world*, the game server has to authenticate your connection (via a join ticket), and that needs a server identity. A purely-local profile can't be trusted by the game server. So we can't delete the anonymous user entirely — but we *can* keep it invisible until the moment you actually go online, which is already the case.

**Where your idea adds something real:** the account row in the DB already stores `name` **and** `skinId` (`db/schema.ts`) — the exact same two fields a profile has. So a logged-in account is *already a profile in disguise*; we just don't treat it as one. The clean version of your idea is: **when logged in, your account acts as your profile; when a guest, the profile lives in the browser.** That removes the confusing "you have a local profile *and* a separate account" duality.

**The one fork that decides the size of the work:**
- Does one account map to **one** profile (its name+skin) — simple, no server changes — or to **many** profiles that **sync across devices** — which needs a new server-side profiles table, API, and a migration? Today the profile list is multi-profile ("who's playing on this browser," family-style); an account is a single identity.
- And: do local single-player *worlds* stay local, or also become account-owned/synced? (There's already a partial cloud-saves feature for single-player worlds.)

Here's my honest recommendation on sequencing, and then I'll let you set the scope:

> AGENT

Of course — what would you like to dig into? A few things I can clarify that might be shaping your thinking:

- **The multi-profile question.** Today the browser can hold several local profiles (a "who's playing on this device" list). If profiles belong to an account, do you still want that — one account holding *several* profiles — or is it simpler in your mind that one account = one identity (one name + skin)? This is the single biggest factor in how much work this is.

- **What "guest" should mean going forward.** Two flavors exist: a purely-offline player (never touches the server) and a "play online without registering" player (needs an invisible server identity to join shared worlds). Do you want guests to be able to play *online* co-op, or is "guest" strictly offline single-player in your vision?

- **Whether worlds follow profiles.** If a profile becomes account-owned, should that profile's *single-player worlds* also sync to the account/cloud, or stay on the device? (There's already a partial cloud-saves feature we could lean on.)

- **Or maybe you just want my recommendation.** If you'd rather I just pick the sanest path and lay it out, I'm happy to — I'd lean toward a clean "account is your profile when logged in, browser holds it when you're a guest" model, fixing the two UX bugs along the way.

What's on your mind?

> DEVELOPER

guest can have multiple local profiles, but only local, he can not create online world and invite others, what do you think?

> AGENT

I like it — that's a clean, common model: a **local/offline guest** vs a **registered account that unlocks online hosting**. And keeping multiple *local* profiles per browser preserves the nice "who's playing on this device" behavior. Two honest caveats, then one pivotal question that decides how much work this is.

**Caveat 1 — this is a deliberate reversal of the current "guests first" design.** Right now guests are first-class online citizens: they can own online worlds *and* invite, and clicking an invite link auto-mints a guest so a friend is playing in seconds (`docs/online.md` → "Identity: guests first"; `app/join/[token]/page.tsx`). Your model gates hosting/inviting behind registration. That's a perfectly legitimate product call — it just partly unwinds a philosophy the whole online stack was built around. Worth doing with eyes open.

**Caveat 2 — gating must be enforced on the server, not just hidden in the UI.** Hiding the "New Online World" / "Invite" buttons isn't enough; the `/api/worlds*` create and invite routes have to *reject* anonymous users, or someone could still hit them directly.

**The pivotal question — can a guest still JOIN someone else's online world?** "Only local" could mean two quite different things:

- **(A) Guest can JOIN but not host.** A guest can accept an invite and play co-op as a participant, they just can't *create* an online world or *invite* others. → We keep the invisible lazy guest identity for joining, and gate only *create + invite* to accounts. Low friction for invited friends; smaller change.
- **(B) Guest is fully offline.** Online is strictly account-only; even accepting an invite forces you to register first. → Guests never get a server identity at all; simpler server model, but a friend must sign up before they can play with you.

Which one matches your intent?

And one thing I want to confirm so I can size it right: it sounds like **profiles just stay local (in the browser) for everyone** — guests *and* logged-in players — and "account" only unlocks *capabilities* (online hosting), rather than becoming the owner/store of your profiles. If that's right, we avoid a server-side profiles table and migration entirely, which keeps this much smaller. Is that the model you have in mind, or do you still want a logged-in account to *own* its profiles (synced across devices)?

> DEVELOPER

guest has local profiles, but online account has online profiles, that sound good, what do you think?

> AGENT

I think it's coherent, and it's a good north star: **guest → multiple local profiles; account → your profile(s) live online and follow you across devices.** Let me give you the honest engineering read, because there's a cheap version and an expensive version of "online profiles," and they differ by a lot.

**The cheap version (my recommendation): one account = one online profile.** Here's the key thing — the account row *already* stores `name` **and** `skinId` (`db/schema.ts`). That's exactly one profile's worth of data, already server-side, already synced across devices for free. So "an account has an online profile" can mean: **your account *is* your online profile.** When you're logged in, the profile-select screen shows your account identity (name + skin, editable) instead of the local list. Near-zero new infrastructure — we just surface `skinId` to the client (it's not in `OnlineUser` today) and let logged-in users edit it. It already syncs everywhere you log in.

**The expensive version: one account = many online profiles.** If a single account needs *several* online identities, we can't hang them off the user row — that needs a new server `profiles` table, CRUD API, a client sync layer, and a localStorage→server migration on upgrade (mirroring how `onLinkAccount` re-parents worlds today). That's a real feature with save-format implications. And honestly — the main reason for *multiple* profiles is "different people share this browser," which the **guest local profiles already handle**. So I'd question whether an account truly needs more than one.

My instinct: **guests keep multiple local profiles; an account is a single online profile (itself).** You get "my profile follows my account" with a fraction of the cost, and you can always add multi-profile-per-account later if it's genuinely wanted.

Two decisions left and I think we can lock the plan:

1. **Can a guest JOIN someone's online world** (accept an invite, play co-op as a participant) but just not *host/invite* — or is a guest **fully offline**, so even joining requires registering first? (You've said guests can't *create/invite*; I want to nail down whether they can *join*.)

2. **Online profiles: one-per-account (my recommendation) or many-per-account?**

And to confirm my sizing assumption: single-player *worlds* stay local (with the existing optional cloud-save) — the "online profile" governs identity + who can host, not where your solo worlds live. Good with that?

> DEVELOPER

guest can not join someone's online world, guest is for the user want play on local, not store information online, what do you think? for online profiles, I think one account can create multiple profiles, but we need add a limit, one account can create 5 maximumn profiles, and one profiles can create 10 maximumn worlds, what do you think? is that good?

> DEVELOPER

guest can not join someone's online world, guest is for the user want play on local, not store information online, what do you think? for online profiles, I think one account can create multiple profiles, but we need add a limit, one account can create 5 maximumn profiles, and one profiles can create 10 maximumn worlds, what do you think? is that good?

> AGENT

Both are good calls. Here's my honest read — then the full model to confirm, because these two answers together commit us to the "full" version *and* quietly retire a whole subsystem.

**"Guest can't join — guest is purely local": yes, and it's actually a net simplification.** But be clear-eyed about what it retires. Today the word "guest" *means* an anonymous **server** user, and the entire online stack is built to let guests own, join, and upgrade: `signIn.anonymous()` + the anonymous plugin, `ensureSignedIn()`, the `onLinkAccount` re-parenting hook, `lib/auth/auth.test.ts`, and the one-click invite in `app/join/[token]/page.tsx`. In your model none of that is needed — online *is* "you have an account." So we **delete the anonymous layer** and **rewire invites to require sign-in first**. Net result: less code and a simpler trust model; the cost is an invited friend must register before playing (no more 10-second guest join). Good trade if "local vs account" is the mental model you want — just naming it. And `docs/online.md` ("Identity: guests first") gets rewritten to "accounts for online, local guest for offline."

**Quotas: sensible, and smart to set them before launch.** 5 profiles × 10 worlds = ≤50 server worlds/account is a fine ceiling. Two notes: (1) enforce on the **server** (the API rejects the 6th profile / 11th world; the UI just shows a friendly "limit reached"), and (2) choosing 5 rather than 1 means we're committing to the real server-side `profiles` table + CRUD + sync + a migration when a local player signs up — the heavier build. Worth it if you want true cloud identities; just confirming, since a cap of 1 would have let the account row *be* the profile with no new table.

**The full model, as I understand it:**
- **Guest (logged out):** many local profiles in the browser, local single-player worlds, zero server. Can't create *or* join anything online.
- **Account (logged in):** up to **5** server-side profiles that sync across devices, each owning up to **10** online worlds; can host, invite, and join.

**One thing to pin before I write it up:** is "local vs online" a clean split — a *logged-in account's worlds are all server-stored* (the 10-world cap counts server worlds), and if you want a purely-local world you just play as a guest? Or should a logged-in user *also* be able to keep local-only worlds? I'd recommend the clean split (**local = guest, online = account**): it's the simplest to reason about, and it lets us build online-profile worlds on top of the **existing single-player cloud-saves plumbing** instead of inventing new storage.

If that split is right, I'll write this as a **phased plan** so you're not facing one giant PR:
- **Phase 1** — the entry-UX fix you originally reported (login reachable up front; a guest can reach the login screen), shippable on its own.
- **Phase 2** — server `profiles` table + quotas + online worlds per profile.
- **Phase 3** — retire the anonymous layer, rewire invites to require login, rewrite docs/tests.

Confirm the local-vs-online split (and that phasing works for you), and I'll write it up.

> DEVELOPER

yes, guest = local single player, may be we do not call it guest, you can suggest what it to be called. logged-in users should not keep local worlds. do you have any other questions?

> AGENT

Good — that fully locks the model. On naming: since the split is really "worlds live in *this browser*" vs "worlds live in *your account*," I'd steer away from "guest" (implies a temporary/lesser online state) and toward something that names the *local* nature. My favorite is **"Local Player"** (entry button "Play Locally," the browser holds "Local Profiles"). I'll put that plus a couple of alternatives in the question below.

Two genuinely plan-shaping questions remain — after these I'm ready to write it up. One clarification first, though: "logged-in users should not keep local worlds" has a gentle and a strict reading, and they're very different for existing players (the game is live, people already have local worlds), so I want to pin exactly which you mean rather than guess. The second is just the name.

One thing I'll *default* rather than ask (tell me if you disagree): existing deployed **guest-owned online worlds** (from the current anonymous-guest system) can't survive the anonymous layer being deleted — I'll plan a migration note / one-time upgrade prompt in Phase 3 rather than silently orphaning them.

```
1	import { betterAuth } from "better-auth";
2	import { drizzleAdapter } from "better-auth/adapters/drizzle";
3	import { anonymous } from "better-auth/plugins";
4	import { eq } from "drizzle-orm";
5	import { db, schema } from "@/db";
6	
7	/**
8	 * Server-side auth. Guests are first-class: the anonymous plugin mints a real
9	 * user (isAnonymous) on the first online action, so "play now" needs no form —
10	 * and when the guest later signs up (email/password or OAuth), onLinkAccount
11	 * re-parents everything they own onto the new account before the anonymous
12	 * user is deleted. That hook IS the "guests keep their worlds" promise; the
13	 * integration test in lib/auth/auth.test.ts exercises it end to end.
14	 *
15	 * Exposed as a factory so tests run the identical wiring against PGlite.
16	 */
17	
18	type AnyDrizzleDb = Parameters<typeof drizzleAdapter>[0];
19	
20	export function createAuth(database: AnyDrizzleDb, options: { baseURL?: string; secret?: string } = {}) {
21	  return betterAuth({
22	    baseURL: options.baseURL ?? process.env.BETTER_AUTH_URL,
23	    secret: options.secret ?? process.env.BETTER_AUTH_SECRET,
24	    database: drizzleAdapter(database, {
25	      provider: "pg",
26	      schema: {
27	        user: schema.user,
28	        session: schema.session,
29	        account: schema.account,
30	        verification: schema.verification
31	      }
32	    }),
33	    emailAndPassword: {
34	      enabled: true
35	    },
36	    user: {
37	      additionalFields: {
38	        skinId: { type: "string", required: false }
39	      }
40	    },
41	    plugins: [
42	      anonymous({
43	        onLinkAccount: async ({ anonymousUser, newUser }) => {
44	          // Everything the guest owned moves to the upgraded account. The
45	          // anonymous user row is deleted right after this hook, and the FKs
46	          // cascade — so a missed table (or a partial failure) would silently
47	          // destroy data. One transaction makes the re-parent all-or-nothing:
48	          // if any statement throws, nothing moves and the guest survives.
49	          const target = database as unknown as import("@/db").Db;
50	          const from = anonymousUser.user.id;
51	          const to = newUser.user.id;
52	          await target.transaction(async (tx) => {
53	            await tx.update(schema.worlds).set({ ownerId: to }).where(eq(schema.worlds.ownerId, from));
54	            await tx.update(schema.worldInvites).set({ createdBy: to }).where(eq(schema.worldInvites.createdBy, from));
55	            // Memberships can collide if the new account already joined the same
56	            // world; drop the guest's row in that case (the membership exists).
57	            const memberships = await tx.select().from(schema.worldMembers).where(eq(schema.worldMembers.userId, from));
58	            for (const membership of memberships) {
59	              await tx
60	                .insert(schema.worldMembers)
61	                .values({ ...membership, userId: to })
62	                .onConflictDoNothing();
63	            }
64	            await tx.delete(schema.worldMembers).where(eq(schema.worldMembers.userId, from));
65	          });
66	        }
67	      })
68	    ]
69	  });
70	}
71	
72	export type Auth = ReturnType<typeof createAuth>;
73	
74	let instance: Auth | null = null;
75	
76	/** The app's auth singleton (lazy: importing this module never needs env). */
77	export function auth(): Auth {
78	  instance ??= createAuth(db());
79	  return instance;
80	}
81
```

```
1	/**
2	 * Player profiles — the top of the save hierarchy. A profile is a distinct
3	 * player (name + appearance) that owns a list of worlds (see lib/game/worlds.ts
4	 * for the 1:N link via `WorldMeta.profileId`). Profiles carry identity only;
5	 * audio settings stay global.
6	 *
7	 * The whole list lives in one versioned localStorage blob. Readers are total:
8	 * malformed entries are dropped, skin ids are sanitized, and a dangling
9	 * `activeProfileId` is repaired to the first surviving profile (or null).
10	 */
11	
12	import { DEFAULT_SKIN_ID, isSkinId, type SkinId } from "./playerSkins";
13	import { readManifestRaw, resolveDeps, sanitizeName, writeManifest, type ManifestDeps } from "./manifest";
14	
15	export type Profile = {
16	  id: string;
17	  name: string;
18	  skinId: SkinId;
19	  createdAt: number;
20	};
21	
22	export type ProfilesManifest = {
23	  version: 1;
24	  profiles: Profile[];
25	  /** The profile to preselect on boot; null shows the create-first-profile flow. */
26	  activeProfileId: string | null;
27	};
28	
29	export const PROFILES_KEY = "minecraft_profiles_v1";
30	export const MAX_PROFILE_NAME = 24;
31	export const DEFAULT_PROFILE_NAME = "Player";
32	
33	export const DEFAULT_PROFILES_MANIFEST: ProfilesManifest = { version: 1, profiles: [], activeProfileId: null };
34	
35	/** Coerces one raw entry into a valid Profile, or null if it can't be salvaged. */
36	function sanitizeProfile(raw: unknown): Profile | null {
37	  if (!raw || typeof raw !== "object") return null;
38	  const entry = raw as Record<string, unknown>;
39	  if (typeof entry.id !== "string" || entry.id.length === 0) return null;
40	  return {
41	    id: entry.id,
42	    name: sanitizeName(entry.name, DEFAULT_PROFILE_NAME, MAX_PROFILE_NAME),
43	    skinId: isSkinId(entry.skinId) ? entry.skinId : DEFAULT_SKIN_ID,
44	    createdAt: typeof entry.createdAt === "number" && Number.isFinite(entry.createdAt) ? entry.createdAt : 0
45	  };
46	}
47	
48	/** Repairs `activeProfileId` so it always names a present profile (or null when empty). */
49	function withRepairedActive(profiles: Profile[], activeProfileId: unknown): ProfilesManifest {
50	  const valid = typeof activeProfileId === "string" && profiles.some((p) => p.id === activeProfileId);
51	  return {
52	    version: 1,
53	    profiles,
54	    activeProfileId: valid ? (activeProfileId as string) : (profiles[0]?.id ?? null)
55	  };
56	}
57	
58	/** Reads the profiles manifest, dropping malformed entries and repairing the active id. Never throws. */
59	export function readProfiles(storage: Storage = localStorage): ProfilesManifest {
60	  const raw = readManifestRaw(PROFILES_KEY, storage) as Partial<ProfilesManifest> | null;
61	  // Only the current manifest version is understood; an unknown version falls
62	  // back to the default rather than risk misreading an incompatible payload.
63	  if (!raw || raw.version !== 1 || !Array.isArray(raw.profiles)) return { ...DEFAULT_PROFILES_MANIFEST };
64	  const profiles = raw.profiles.map(sanitizeProfile).filter((p): p is Profile => p !== null);
65	  return withRepairedActive(profiles, raw.activeProfileId);
66	}
67	
68	export function getProfile(id: string, storage: Storage = localStorage): Profile | null {
69	  return readProfiles(storage).profiles.find((p) => p.id === id) ?? null;
70	}
71	
72	export function getActiveProfile(storage: Storage = localStorage): Profile | null {
73	  const manifest = readProfiles(storage);
74	  return manifest.profiles.find((p) => p.id === manifest.activeProfileId) ?? null;
75	}
76	
77	/** Creates a profile, makes it the active one, persists, and returns it. */
78	export function createProfile(name: string, skinId: SkinId, deps: ManifestDeps = {}): Profile {
79	  const { storage, now, uid } = resolveDeps(deps);
80	  const profile: Profile = {
81	    id: uid(),
82	    name: sanitizeName(name, DEFAULT_PROFILE_NAME, MAX_PROFILE_NAME),
83	    skinId: isSkinId(skinId) ? skinId : DEFAULT_SKIN_ID,
84	    createdAt: now()
85	  };
86	  const manifest = readProfiles(storage);
87	  const next: ProfilesManifest = { version: 1, profiles: [...manifest.profiles, profile], activeProfileId: profile.id };
88	  writeManifest(PROFILES_KEY, next, storage);
89	  return profile;
90	}
91	
92	export function renameProfile(id: string, name: string, storage: Storage = localStorage): ProfilesManifest {
93	  const manifest = readProfiles(storage);
94	  const next = withRepairedActive(
95	    manifest.profiles.map((p) => (p.id === id ? { ...p, name: sanitizeName(name, p.name, MAX_PROFILE_NAME) } : p)),
96	    manifest.activeProfileId
97	  );
98	  writeManifest(PROFILES_KEY, next, storage);
99	  return next;
100	}
101	
102	export function setProfileSkin(id: string, skinId: SkinId, storage: Storage = localStorage): ProfilesManifest {
103	  const manifest = readProfiles(storage);
104	  const safeSkin = isSkinId(skinId) ? skinId : DEFAULT_SKIN_ID;
105	  const next = withRepairedActive(
106	    manifest.profiles.map((p) => (p.id === id ? { ...p, skinId: safeSkin } : p)),
107	    manifest.activeProfileId
108	  );
109	  writeManifest(PROFILES_KEY, next, storage);
110	  return next;
111	}
112	
113	export function setActiveProfile(id: string | null, storage: Storage = localStorage): ProfilesManifest {
114	  const manifest = readProfiles(storage);
115	  const next = withRepairedActive(manifest.profiles, id);
116	  writeManifest(PROFILES_KEY, next, storage);
117	  return next;
118	}
119	
120	/**
121	 * Removes a profile and repairs the active id. Does NOT touch the profile's
122	 * worlds — call `deleteWorldsForProfile` (lib/game/worlds.ts) first so the
123	 * world records and their save blobs are reclaimed too.
124	 */
125	export function deleteProfile(id: string, storage: Storage = localStorage): ProfilesManifest {
126	  const manifest = readProfiles(storage);
127	  const profiles = manifest.profiles.filter((p) => p.id !== id);
128	  const next = withRepairedActive(profiles, manifest.activeProfileId === id ? null : manifest.activeProfileId);
129	  writeManifest(PROFILES_KEY, next, storage);
130	  return next;
131	}
132	
```

```
1	import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from "react";
2	import CreateWorldForm from "@/components/menu/CreateWorldForm";
3	import MenuScreen from "@/components/menu/MenuScreen";
4	import type { Profile } from "@/lib/game/profiles";
5	import { GAME_MODE_PRESETS, type GameMode } from "@/lib/game/gameModes";
6	import { DIFFICULTY_PRESETS, type Difficulty } from "@/lib/game/difficulties";
7	import {
8	  createWorld,
9	  deleteWorld,
10	  linkWorldCloud,
11	  MAX_WORLD_NAME,
12	  renameWorld,
13	  WORLD_TYPE_PRESETS,
14	  worldSaveKey,
15	  worldsForProfile,
16	  type WorldMeta
17	} from "@/lib/game/worlds";
18	import { readSave } from "@/lib/game/save";
19	import { pushSave } from "@/lib/game/cloudSaves";
20	import type { WorldType } from "@/lib/world";
21	import { onlineUsed } from "@/lib/auth/client";
22	import { createInviteLink, createOnlineWorld, listOnlineWorlds, revokeInviteLinks, type OnlineWorld } from "@/lib/online/onlineClient";
23	import { resolveSeed } from "@/lib/game/worlds";
24	
25	/** Short label for a world type (the default type is left unlabelled on cards). */
26	function worldTypeLabel(id: WorldType): string {
27	  return WORLD_TYPE_PRESETS.find((preset) => preset.id === id)?.label ?? id;
28	}
29	
30	/** Short label for a game mode (survival is left unlabelled on cards). */
31	function gameModeLabel(id: GameMode): string {
32	  return GAME_MODE_PRESETS.find((preset) => preset.id === id)?.label ?? id;
33	}
34	
35	/** Short label for a difficulty (normal is left unlabelled on cards). */
36	function difficultyLabel(id: Difficulty): string {
37	  return DIFFICULTY_PRESETS.find((preset) => preset.id === id)?.label ?? id;
38	}
39	
40	type WorldSelectProps = {
41	  profile: Profile;
42	  /** Enter a world (the shell records last-played and boots it). */
43	  onPlay: (worldId: string) => void;
44	  /** Join an online (server-hosted) world. */
45	  onPlayOnline: (world: OnlineWorld) => void;
46	  /** Materialize a cloud save (sp-cloud) as a local world and open it. */
47	  onDownloadCloud: (world: OnlineWorld) => void;
48	  /** Back to the profile list. */
49	  onBack: () => void;
50	};
51	
52	/** A profile's world list: pick a world, or create / rename / delete one. */
53	export default function WorldSelect({ profile, onPlay, onPlayOnline, onDownloadCloud, onBack }: WorldSelectProps) {
54	  const [creating, setCreating] = useState(false);
55	  const [creatingOnline, setCreatingOnline] = useState(false);
56	  const [onlineWorlds, setOnlineWorlds] = useState<OnlineWorld[] | null>(null);
57	  const [inviteCopied, setInviteCopied] = useState<string | null>(null);
58	  const [inviteError, setInviteError] = useState<string | null>(null);
59	  const [invitesRevoked, setInvitesRevoked] = useState<string | null>(null);
60	  const [onlineCreateError, setOnlineCreateError] = useState<string | null>(null);
61	  // Per-world (not single scalars): uploads run independently, so tracking one
62	  // id would re-enable another card's button mid-flight and allow a double-upload.
63	  const [uploading, setUploading] = useState<ReadonlySet<string>>(() => new Set());
64	  const [uploadError, setUploadError] = useState<ReadonlySet<string>>(() => new Set());
65	
66	  // Online worlds appear only once this browser has used online features —
67	  // offline-first: no fetch, no section, no account until the player opts in.
68	  const refreshOnline = useCallback(() => {
69	    if (onlineUsed()) void listOnlineWorlds().then(setOnlineWorlds);
70	  }, []);
71	  useEffect(() => refreshOnline(), [refreshOnline]);
72	  const [editingId, setEditingId] = useState<string | null>(null);
73	  const [editName, setEditName] = useState("");
74	  const [confirmingDeleteId, setConfirmingDeleteId] = useState<string | null>(null);
75	
76	  if (creating) {
77	    return (
78	      <MenuScreen title={`${profile.name} — New World`}>
79	        <CreateWorldForm
80	          onCreate={(name, seed, worldType, gameMode, difficulty, hardcore) => {
81	            const world = createWorld(profile.id, name, seed, { worldType, gameMode, difficulty, hardcore });
82	            setCreating(false);
83	            onPlay(world.id); // straight into the freshly created world
84	          }}
85	          onCancel={() => setCreating(false)}
86	        />
87	      </MenuScreen>
88	    );
89	  }
90	
91	  if (creatingOnline) {
92	    return (
93	      <MenuScreen title={`${profile.name} — New Online World`}>
94	        {onlineCreateError && <p className="account-error">{onlineCreateError}</p>}
95	        <CreateWorldForm
96	          onCreate={(name, seed, worldType, gameMode, difficulty, hardcore) => {
97	            // Same form, different home: the world row lives on the server and
98	            // the game server hosts it — friends join by invite link.
99	            setOnlineCreateError(null);
100	            void createOnlineWorld({ name, seed: resolveSeed(seed), worldType, gameMode, difficulty, hardcore }).then((world) => {
101	              if (world) {
102	                setCreatingOnline(false);
103	                onPlayOnline(world);
104	              } else {
105	                // Keep the form open so the entered settings aren't lost on a retry.
106	                setOnlineCreateError("Couldn't create the online world — are you signed in and online?");
107	              }
108	            });
109	          }}
110	          onCancel={() => setCreatingOnline(false)}
111	        />
112	      </MenuScreen>
113	    );
114	  }
115	
116	  const worlds = worldsForProfile(profile.id);
117	
118	  const without = (set: ReadonlySet<string>, id: string) => {
119	    const next = new Set(set);
120	    next.delete(id);
121	    return next;
122	  };
123	
124	  // "Upload to cloud": create an sp-cloud world row, push the current save, and —
125	  // only if the push actually lands — link it so the card flips to "Synced".
126	  // Linking on a failed push would falsely claim the (empty) cloud row is synced.
127	  const uploadToCloud = (world: WorldMeta) => {
128	    setUploadError((prev) => without(prev, world.id));
129	    setUploading((prev) => new Set(prev).add(world.id));
130	    const save = readSave(worldSaveKey(world.id));
131	    void createOnlineWorld({
132	      name: world.name,
133	      seed: world.seed,
134	      worldType: world.worldType,
135	      gameMode: world.gameMode,
136	      difficulty: world.difficulty,
137	      hardcore: world.hardcore,
138	      kind: "sp-cloud"
139	    }).then(async (cloud) => {
140	      // A world with no local save yet has nothing to push — link it now and let
141	      // the first play autosave upload the blob.
142	      const pushed = cloud && save ? await pushSave(cloud.id, save) : "saved";
143	      if (cloud && pushed === "saved") {
144	        linkWorldCloud(world.id, cloud.id);
145	        refreshOnline();
146	      } else {
147	        setUploadError((prev) => new Set(prev).add(world.id)); // keep the world local — surface the failure
148	      }
149	      setUploading((prev) => without(prev, world.id));
150	    });
151	  };
152	
153	  // Split the server world list: mp rooms stay in Online Worlds; sp-cloud saves
154	  // not yet on this device become downloadable in the Cloud Saves section.
155	  const mpWorlds = onlineWorlds?.filter((world) => world.kind === "mp") ?? [];
156	  const linkedCloudIds = new Set(worlds.map((world) => world.cloudId).filter((id): id is string => Boolean(id)));
157	  const cloudWorlds = onlineWorlds?.filter((world) => world.kind === "sp-cloud" && !linkedCloudIds.has(world.id)) ?? [];
158	
159	  return (
160	    <MenuScreen title={`${profile.name} — Worlds`}>
161	      {worlds.length === 0 ? (
162	        <p className="menu-empty">No worlds yet. Create your first world.</p>
163	      ) : (
164	        <ul className="menu-list">
165	          {worlds.map((world) => (
166	            <li key={world.id} className="menu-card">
167	              {editingId === world.id ? (
168	                <form
169	                  className="menu-rename"
170	                  onSubmit={(event) => {
171	                    event.preventDefault();
172	                    renameWorld(world.id, editName);
173	                    setEditingId(null); // re-render re-reads the manifest
174	                  }}
175	                >
176	                  <input
177	                    className="menu-input"
178	                    value={editName}
179	                    maxLength={MAX_WORLD_NAME}
180	                    autoFocus
181	                    aria-label="Rename world"
182	                    onChange={(event) => setEditName(event.target.value)}
183	                  />
184	                  <button type="submit" className="mc-button">
185	                    Save
186	                  </button>
187	                  <button type="button" className="mc-button" onClick={() => setEditingId(null)}>
188	                    Cancel
189	                  </button>
190	                </form>
191	              ) : confirmingDeleteId === world.id ? (
192	                <div className="menu-confirm">
193	                  <span>Delete “{world.name}”? This cannot be undone.</span>
194	                  <div className="menu-confirm-actions">
195	                    <button
196	                      className="mc-button danger"
197	                      onClick={() => {
198	                        deleteWorld(world.id);
199	                        setConfirmingDeleteId(null); // re-render re-reads the manifest
200	                      }}
201	                    >
202	                      Delete
203	                    </button>
204	                    <button className="mc-button" onClick={() => setConfirmingDeleteId(null)}>
205	                      Cancel
206	                    </button>
207	                  </div>
208	                </div>
209	              ) : (
210	                <>
211	                  <button className="menu-card-play" data-testid={`world-${world.id}`} onClick={() => onPlay(world.id)}>
212	                    <span className="menu-card-name">{world.name}</span>
213	                    <span className="menu-card-sub">
214	                      {world.hardcore ? `Hardcore · ` : ""}
215	                      {world.gameMode !== "survival" ? `${gameModeLabel(world.gameMode)} · ` : ""}
216	                      {!world.hardcore && world.difficulty !== "normal" ? `${difficultyLabel(world.difficulty)} · ` : ""}
217	                      {world.worldType !== "default" ? `${worldTypeLabel(world.worldType)} · ` : ""}Seed {world.seed}
218	                    </span>
219	                  </button>
220	                  <div className="menu-card-actions">
221	                    {onlineUsed() &&
222	                      (world.cloudId ? (
223	                        <span className="menu-cloud-badge" title="This world syncs to your account across devices">
224	                          ☁ Synced
225	                        </span>
226	                      ) : (
227	                        <button
228	                          className="mc-button"
229	                          disabled={uploading.has(world.id)}
230	                          title="Sync this world to your account so it follows you across devices"
231	                          onClick={() => uploadToCloud(world)}
232	                        >
233	                          {uploading.has(world.id) ? "Uploading…" : uploadError.has(world.id) ? "Upload failed" : "Upload to cloud"}
234	                        </button>
235	                      ))}
236	                    <button
237	                      className="mc-button"
238	                      onClick={() => {
239	                        setEditName(world.name);
240	                        setEditingId(world.id);
241	                      }}
242	                    >
243	                      Rename
244	                    </button>
245	                    <button className="mc-button" onClick={() => setConfirmingDeleteId(world.id)}>
246	                      Delete
247	                    </button>
248	                  </div>
249	                </>
250	              )}
251	            </li>
252	          ))}
253	        </ul>
254	      )}
255	      {cloudWorlds.length > 0 && (
256	        <section className="menu-online">
257	          <h3 className="menu-online-title">Cloud Saves</h3>
258	          <ul className="menu-list">
259	            {cloudWorlds.map((world) => (
260	              <li key={world.id} className="menu-card">
261	                <button className="menu-card-play" data-testid={`cloud-world-${world.id}`} onClick={() => onDownloadCloud(world)}>
262	                  <span className="menu-card-name">{world.name}</span>
263	                  <span className="menu-card-sub">Download to this device · Seed {world.seed}</span>
264	                </button>
265	              </li>
266	            ))}
267	          </ul>
268	        </section>
269	      )}
270	      {onlineWorlds !== null && (
271	        <section className="menu-online">
272	          <h3 className="menu-online-title">Online Worlds</h3>
273	          {mpWorlds.length === 0 ? (
274	            <p className="menu-empty">No online worlds yet — create one and share the invite link.</p>
275	          ) : (
276	            <ul className="menu-list">
277	              {mpWorlds.map((world) => (
278	                <li key={world.id} className="menu-card">
279	                  <button className="menu-card-play" data-testid={`online-world-${world.id}`} onClick={() => onPlayOnline(world)}>
280	                    <span className="menu-card-name">{world.name}</span>
281	                    <span className="menu-card-sub">
282	                      {world.role === "owner" ? "Your world" : "Joined"} · Seed {world.seed}
283	                    </span>
284	                  </button>
285	                  {world.role === "owner" && (
286	                    <div className="menu-card-actions">
287	                      <button
288	                        className="mc-button"
289	                        onClick={() => {
290	                          setInvitesRevoked(null);
291	                          setInviteError(null);
292	                          setInviteCopied(null);
293	                          void createInviteLink(world.id).then((link) => {
```

```json
  "content": "# Rework the identity model: Local Players vs Accounts\n\n## Context\n\nMultiplayer works, but the account/profile onboarding is backwards and incomplete, and the underlying identity model doesn't match how we want the game to feel:\n\n- **Login is blocked behind profile creation.** On first run (`components/menu/ProfileSelect.tsx:27`), the zero-profiles branch renders `CreateProfileForm` full-screen and *hides* `<AccountPanel/>` (the Sign in / Register / online controls, only rendered in the profile-list view at `ProfileSelect.tsx:44`). A brand-new player literally cannot reach login/register until a local profile exists.\n- **Guest mode is a one-way street.** In `components/menu/AccountPanel.tsx:94-97` a guest (`user.isAnonymous`) is offered *only* \"Keep my worlds — create account\". `signOut()` exists solely in the non-anonymous branch (`:103`), so a guest can never get back to the login screen.\n\nBeyond the two bugs, we're deliberately **redefining the identity model** (this reverses the current \"guests first\" design in `docs/online.md`):\n\n- **Local Player** (logged out): keeps many **local profiles** in the browser (localStorage, exactly as today), owns local single-player worlds, makes **zero** server calls. Cannot create, invite to, *or join* anything online. Entry point is **\"Play Locally\"**; the list is **\"Local Profiles\"**.\n- **Account** (logged in): owns up to **5 online profiles** stored server-side that sync across devices; each profile owns up to **10 online worlds**; can host, invite, and join. When logged in you see **only** your account's online profiles and their worlds — existing local worlds are **hidden but preserved** (never deleted, never auto-uploaded), and reappear when you play as a Local Player again.\n\nGuests-as-anonymous-server-users go away entirely: online == you have an account.\n\n**Sequencing:** ship as three landable phases. Phase 1 fixes the two reported bugs on its own and is low-risk. Phases 2–3 build the new model and retire the guest layer.\n\n---\n\n## Target model (agreed rules)\n\n| | Local Player (logged out) | Account (logged in) |\n|---|---|---|\n| Profiles | many, local (localStorage) | up to **5**, server-side, synced |\n| Worlds | local single-player only | online only, up to **10 per profile** |\n| Online create / invite / join | ❌ none | ✅ |\n| Storage | browser only, no network | server (`worlds` rows + `saveBlob`) |\n| On login | local worlds **hidden but preserved** | shows only account's online profiles/worlds |\n\n---\n\n## Phase 1 — Entry-UX fix (the two reported bugs) · independently shippable\n\nSmall, low-risk, no data-model change. Keeps today's backend semantics.\n\n1. **Login reachable on first run** — `components/menu/ProfileSelect.tsx`. Render `<AccountPanel/>` in the first-run (`firstRun`) branch too, above the create-profile form, so Sign in / Register are always reachable. Safe for offline-first: `AccountPanel`'s effect only calls the server when `onlineUsed()` is true (`AccountPanel.tsx:24`), so a brand-new offline player still makes no auth request.\n2. **Guest can return to login** — `components/menu/AccountPanel.tsx:94-97`. In the `user.isAnonymous` branch add a **\"Sign out\"** button mirroring the existing non-anonymous handler (`:102-105`: `await authClient().signOut(); await refresh();`), which drops back to the \"Offline\" state where \"Sign in\" / \"Play online as guest\" already live.\n3. **Tests** — update `components/menu/ProfileSelect.test.tsx` (the \"no profiles → no cancel\" test now also expects the account panel present) and `components/menu/AccountPanel.test.tsx` (add: a guest can sign out and returns to \"Offline\").\n4. **Docs/changelog** — `CHANGELOG.md` entry; note the fix in `docs/online.md`.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2 — Local vs Account identity, server profiles, quotas\n\nThe core feature. Introduces a server-side profile entity and makes the menu auth-aware.\n\n### Server: profiles table + per-profile worlds\n- **Schema** — add a `profiles` table to `db/schema.ts` and `db/ddl.ts` (mirror `user`/`worlds` style): `{ id, ownerId → user.id (cascade), name, skinId, createdAt }`. Add a nullable `profileId → profiles.id` column to the `worlds` table (the account still owns via `ownerId`; `profileId` says *which* profile within the account). Add a drizzle migration under `db/migrations`, and a data backfill: for each existing account create one default profile (from the account's `name` + `skinId`) and stamp its existing worlds with that `profileId`.\n- **Service + quotas** — in `lib/online/worldsService.ts`: add profile CRUD (`listProfiles`/`createProfile`/`renameProfile`/`setProfileSkin`/`deleteProfile`, scoped to `ownerId`) and enforce quotas at write time — reject the 6th profile per account and the 11th world per profile (count-before-insert, returning the existing `failure`/`STATUS` shape). Put the limits in `config.ts` (`MAX_ONLINE_PROFILES = 5`, `MAX_WORLDS_PER_PROFILE = 10`) so they're documented in `docs/tuning.md`.\n- **Routes** — add `app/api/profiles/route.ts` (GET list / POST create) and `app/api/profiles/[id]/route.ts` (PATCH / DELETE), thin adapters over the service using the existing `sessionUser` + `unauthorized()` pattern from `lib/online/http.ts`.\n- **Gate online to accounts** — surface `isAnonymous` in `sessionUser` (`lib/online/http.ts` currently drops it) and reject anonymous users in `createWorld`, `createInvite`, and `mintTicket` (`worldsService.ts`). One choke point; routes need no change.\n- **Ticket identity = the profile** — `mintTicket` should stamp `name`/`skinId` from the *selected profile*, not the account. The ticket route (`app/api/worlds/[id]/ticket/route.ts`) takes a `profileId`, validated to belong to the caller. (Decide during impl whether the game server's per-player save slice keys on account `sub` or on `profileId` — see Open decisions.)\n\n### Client\n- **Online profiles wrapper** — new `lib/online/profilesClient.ts` mirroring `lib/online/onlineClient.ts` (total functions returning `null`/`false`): `listOnlineProfiles`, `createOnlineProfile`, `renameOnlineProfile`, `deleteOnlineProfile`.\n- **Auth-aware ProfileSelect** — `components/menu/ProfileSelect.tsx` becomes the hub. Logged out → local profiles from `lib/game/profiles.ts` under a **\"Play Locally\"** heading (unchanged behavior). Logged in → fetch and list **online profiles**; local worlds are simply not shown. The account panel gains a clear **Sign out** → returns to local mode. Rename UI copy: guest/\"Play online as guest\" → \"Play Locally\" / \"Sign in / Register\".\n- **World lists** — `components/menu/WorldSelect.tsx` already splits local / Cloud Saves / Online Worlds. In account mode it shows the selected online profile's worlds (server `worlds` filtered by `profileId`), reusing `createOnlineWorld`/`listOnlineWorlds`/invite helpers. The existing `sp-cloud` cloud-save storage layer (`lib/game/cloudSaves.ts`, `putSaveBlob`/`getSaveBlob`) is the storage for account worlds — no new blob path needed.\n- **GameShell wiring** — `components/GameShell.tsx` threads the selected profile (local `Profile` or online profile) into play, and passes `profileId` to `playOnline` → ticket request.\n\n### Tests\n- `lib/online/worldsService.test.ts` (or existing service tests): quota enforcement (6th profile, 11th world), anonymous rejection on create/invite/ticket, ticket carries profile identity.\n- Component tests for the auth-aware `ProfileSelect` (local vs account mode) and the new profiles client.\n- Update the coverage map in `docs/testing.md`.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3 — Retire the anonymous-guest layer, rewire invites, migrate, docs\n\n1. **Remove anonymous auth** — delete the `anonymous()` plugin + `onLinkAccount` re-parenting (`lib/auth/server.ts:41-68`), `anonymousClient()` and the `ensureSignedIn` anonymous path (`lib/auth/client.ts:22,58-65`), the `isAnonymous` column (`db/schema.ts:32`, `db/ddl.ts`), and the guest branches in `AccountPanel.tsx`. The game server and ticket format need no change (they never referenced anonymity).\n2. **Invites require sign-in** — `app/join/[token]/page.tsx:26` currently calls `ensureSignedIn()` (mints a guest). Change unauthenticated visitors to a real Sign in / Register step (the world name still previews via the no-auth `GET /api/invite/:token`), then accept the invite, then **pick which profile** joins.\n3. **Migration of existing deployed data** — accounts and their worlds are safe (backfilled in Phase 2). Existing **guest-owned** online worlds/memberships can't survive removing anonymous users; default plan: drop them via the migration (or leave orphaned) and call it out in release notes. Existing **local** profiles/worlds are untouched.\n4. **Docs** — rewrite `docs/online.md` \"Identity: guests first\" → \"accounts for online, Local Players for offline\"; update `docs/save-format.md` (server profiles, per-profile worlds), `docs/protocol.md` (ticket carries profile identity), `docs/reference.md`/`docs/manual.md` (quotas, Play Locally), and `CHANGELOG.md`. Remove/replace `lib/auth/auth.test.ts` (the onLinkAccount test) and the guest-path e2e in `e2e/multiplayer.e2e.ts`.\n\n---\n\n## Verification\n\n- **Per phase:** `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run format:check`, `bun test`, `bun run build` (the CI `verify` gate); `bun run test:e2e` for Phases touching the React shell / renderer (all three).\n- **Phase 1 manual:** fresh browser (clear localStorage) → first screen shows both Sign in / Register **and** the create-profile form (login no longer blocked). Enter guest → a \"Sign out\" returns to the login screen.\n- **Phase 2 manual (zero-service online):** run with `DATABASE_URL=pglite://memory` + `PERSISTENCE=memory` (`docs/online.md` \"No Docker at all\"): sign in → see online profiles (not local worlds); create a 6th profile → rejected; create an 11th world under a profile → rejected; local worlds reappear after Sign out.\n- **Phase 3 manual:** an invite link while logged out routes to Sign in / Register (no silent guest); after signing in and picking a profile, the world joins. Confirm no `signIn.anonymous` path remains (grep).\n\n## Open decisions / risks (resolve during implementation)\n\n- **Save-slice keying for shared MP worlds:** account (`sub`) vs `profileId`. Simplest MVP keys by account; profile-level identity is the \"purer\" model but touches save v-format + the game server. Decide in Phase 2.\n- **Existing guest-owned online worlds** on the live deployment: confirm \"drop with release-note warning\" is acceptable, or add a one-time upgrade prompt before Phase 3 ships.\n- **Offline-first invariant** stays intact: no server call for a logged-out Local Player (keep the `onlineUsed()` gate before any session fetch).\n"
```

> AGENT

Good question — for a change this size, git strategy matters. My honest take:

**One branch + several commits + one PR is the right shape for the redesign** — this is exactly the "big work → its own branch, lands as one reviewable unit" case from `AGENTS.md`. One caveat and one refinement:

- **Phases 2 and 3 should land *together*** (same branch, same PR). If Phase 2 ships without Phase 3, production sits in a half-migrated state — the anonymous-guest layer is still minting guests (e.g. the invite flow) while the new account-profiles model is already live and gating online to non-anonymous. That intermediate state is awkward and not something you want deployed. Landing them together avoids it. Within the PR they're still cleanly separated commits (schema+migration → service+quotas → routes → client/UI → remove-anonymous → invites → docs).

- **Phase 1 (the two reported bugs) I'd ship first, separately.** It's a pure UI bug fix with no data-model change and it fixes something live users hit *today* — no reason to make it wait behind the multi-day redesign. Per your own workflow it even qualifies as "minor work → straight to `main`", but I'd lean to a small focused PR for visibility. Your call.
